    38→Stage Summary:
    39→- Scripts: 4/5 are genuinely useful (evaluate.py trains+saves+registers model; cost_table.py produces cost_table.md; security_probes.py is a real authz/PII/idempotency probe harness; demo_agent.py is a working narrative demo with abuse drills). ingest_kaggle.py is the weakest — manual Kaggle CSV download, writes data/raw/ingested_real.csv, but warns that "load_orders" must be extended to read it (so the wiring is unfinished).
    40→- Dashboard: vanilla JS, no framework, no chart lib — bars and gauge are hand-rolled CSS. It IS genuinely wired to /risk/score and /audit/{id} (not mocked). But "scored orders this session" is in-memory only (no persistence, lost on refresh), and the cost-threshold explorer uses a HARDCODED array `COSTS=[[0.15,1258],...]` — not fetched from /v1/policy/optimal. Slick-looking but half-toy.
    41→- verify.sh: ruff + pytest + evaluate.py. Hardcoded interpreter path `/mnt/20265E15265DEC72/...linux_venv/bin/python` (author's machine only — will not run anywhere else). No CI config present in repo.
    42→- docker-compose: api (build) + nginx/redis/postgres/prometheus/grafana all under `profile: full`. Postgres has no volumes, no migrations, no healthcheck. Redis is declared but UNUSED (no service depends on it, no env wiring). Grafana volume path is wrong (`/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards-src` is not a standard Grafana path; should be `/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards`).
    43→- Dockerfile: single-stage python:3.12-slim, copies src/scripts/dashboard/tests but NOT data/raw except cod_orders.csv. Bakes default ENV secrets (change-me-scorer / rotate-in-prod) — defaults are placeholders but visible in image layers.
    44→- nginx.conf: rate-limit 25r/s burst 50, /metrics gated to private CIDRs, /dashboard proxied, no TLS, no cache headers, no security headers (CSP/HSTS/XFO).
    45→- Monitoring: prometheus.yml scrapes only api:8000/metrics (no postgres/redis/nginx exporters). Grafana dashboard has 4 panels (Decisions/min by outcome, Circuit breaker state, Degraded share, Scoring latency avg). No alerting rules. Looks minimal but real (metric names risk_decisions_total, rto_circuit_state, rto_score_latency_seconds — these are referenced from src/api/metrics.py per the openapi /metrics endpoint).
    46→- pyproject.toml vs requirements.txt: NOT in sync. pyproject.toml has ONLY [tool.ruff] config, no [project] table, no dependencies. requirements.txt is the source of truth. uv.lock is a 3-line stub (version=1, revision=3, requires-python>=3.11) — NOT a real lockfile, uv was never run. No dev/runtime split. Pin discipline is loose (>=).
    47→- openapi.json: 16 endpoints, auto-generated from FastAPI, recent and detailed (matches v0.4 platform-complete state per autoresearch TSV).
    48→- autoresearch-results.tsv: 6 iterations, looks like an autonomous orchestrator's research loop trace (decision kept/discarded, metric_value = pr_auc 0.524→0.5495, hypothesis, change_summary, branch, parent_id). Iteration 5 mentions "5/5 probes mitigated", iteration 6 = v0.4 platform-complete with "41 tests green". This is the project's own build history, not external research.
    49→- Gaps vs production: no CI/CD workflow file in repo (verify.sh references a venv path), no secrets manager, no DB migrations runner (no alembic/flyway), no message bus (kafka/redis-streams) — Redis is declared but unused, Postgres has no schema, no object store, no tracing (no OTel), no log aggregation, no alerting rules in Prometheus/Grafana, no runbook, infra/README.md explicitly defers IaC ("intentionally contains no half-deployable Terraform").

    69→Stage Summary:
    70→- Confirmed framing: COD RTO (Return-to-Origin) risk scoring for Indian e-commerce; target customer = Razorpay AI Buildathon Track 02 (AI Risk Manager).
    71→- Architecture truth = V3 (DRAFT-FOR-REVIEW); V1 is the model-only snapshot; V2 is explicitly superseded but infra/README.md still points at V2 §9.2 as production spec — stale reference, contradicts V3.
    72→- V3 is unusually self-aware: §1 audit A1-A19 rejects ~80% of V2's enterprise boxes (Kafka, ClickHouse, Feast, MLflow-server, Hyperledger Aries, E2B sandbox, Listmonk, Go/Rust rules) as cargo-cult / resume-driven / license-inconsistent; flags V2 patent numbers as SUSPECT-FABRICATED.
    73→- Real code footprint in src/: routes.py, mandates.py, breaker.py, engine.py, logger.py, cost_optimizer.py, cases/service.py, ml/registry.py, audit/logger.py, api/{security,metrics,mandates,breaker,routes}.py + dashboard/index.html — V3 §20 says only 12 code deltas (CD-1..CD-12) needed to close plan↔code gap.
    74→- API surface = 16 endpoints, all exist (openapi.json matches API_SPEC.md). But API_SPEC.md is path-list-only; no request/response bodies, no examples — looks mid vs MS Fabric reference.
    75→- Cost model = simple 8-row table, FN=12×FP, optimal threshold 0.15. V3 §11.6 promises to extend into full intervention-policy engine {ship, otp_verify, partial_cod, address_check, hold} — aspirational, not in code.
    76→- Feature importance = 10 features, permutation AP drop, is_cod dominates (0.18) then PriorReturns (0.115). OrderDay/OrderHour negative (noise). Tied to real E2 model run via scripts/evaluate.py — NOT aspirational.
    77→- Pitch = single-author demo ("I'm Neeraj"); customer=Razorpay judges; asks: real labeled data + pincode dir + Agent Studio integration. No monetization/pricing ask — pure buildathon entry.
    78→- Research bibliography strong (18 papers, incl. 2026 DOI-anchored anchor paper on AI Agents in Payments); but only 3 of 18 PDFs committed (Tramer/NIST/Fraud-RLA); the `paper studied/` KB mentioned in worklog header is DIFFERENT from docs/research/ — docs/research/ is the project's own citations, NOT the user's 40-paper distillation.
    79→- Top gaps/smells: (1) API_SPEC.md has no schemas/examples — judges will judge by this file first; (2) infra/README.md points at superseded V2; (3) compose `--profile full` starts Postgres+Redis that nothing wires to (V3 finding A2); (4) model trains-at-startup inside API (V3 A3, CD-1); (5) audit is fire-and-forget BackgroundTasks violating RPO=0 (V3 A5, CD-3/CD-4); (6) Merkle proof endpoint /v1/audit/{id}/proof is specified in V3 §10.3 but NOT in openapi.json (16 paths) — V3 spec > code; (7) /v1/simulate, /v1/usage, /v1/cases vs /v1/cases — V3 mentions many endpoints not yet in API; (8) no real IaC, no Alembic, no SDK clients published; (9) "16 paths" but no examples — comparing to MS Fabric fraud reference, missing: real-time ingestion pipeline diagram, eventhouse/lakehouse layer, KQL queries, PowerBI dashboard artifact, activation loop.

    98→Stage Summary:
    99→- App: FastAPI factory `create_app()` in src/api/routes.py (524 LOC, single file doing routes + decision pipeline + audit + metrics + dashboard mount). No ASGI entrypoint file in src/. Lifespan loads CSV `data/raw/cod_orders.csv` -> trains HistGradientBoostingClassifier in-process -> dumps to `out/model_api.joblib` if missing. No middleware wired (no CORS, no TrustedHost, no GZip, no OTel middleware).
   100→- Real working subsystems: hash-chained JSONL audit (`AuditLogger.verify_chain`, with byte-offset index), HMAC-signed mandates with VALID/TAMPERED/EXPIRED/BREACH verdicts, in-memory thread-safe circuit breaker (CLOSED/OPEN/HALF_OPEN) protecting model.predict_proba only, rules engine with 4 ops (gt/lt/eq/in) + 2 default rules + short-circuit on first match, token-bucket rate limiter, idempotency cache (unbounded), PSI drift calc, permutation-importance + leave-one-out local attribution (NOT SHAP despite shap in requirements.txt — dead dep).
   101→- Stubs / half-implementations: CaseService._latest returns None always (line 64 dead placeholder); no DB anywhere (everything is JSONL/CSV files); register_model never called from runtime so `current_champion()` returns None and model-card endpoint hardcodes fallback `"dev"`; cost_optimizer is only attached as a `policy_hint` field, NOT used in the actual ACCEPT/REVIEW/REJECT decision (routes.py:172-180 uses static thresholds 0.15/0.60); no A/B, no shadow deploy, no challenger promotion path; features are pure toy (no device fingerprint / velocity / graph features, only 8 numeric + 3 categorical).
   102→- Security: 2 static demo keys hardcoded as fallback ("score-demo-key", "admin-demo-key") with sha256-equality check; no JWT, no per-merchant RBAC, no nonce/replay protection beyond idempotency-key, no HMAC on requests (mandates are the only HMAC). Secrets from env: RTO_SCORER_KEYS, RTO_ADMIN_KEYS, RTO_MANDATE_SECRET, RTO_AUDIT_SALT.
   103→- Tests: 5 real pytest files (~526 LOC), actually assert behavior (hash-chain tamper detection, mandate breach escalation, circuit breaker open, degraded mode, RBAC scopes). k6 load profile (steady+ramp+spike, p99<400ms threshold). No CI config visible in scope.
   104→- Critical production gaps vs Microsoft Fabric fraud ref: no DB/migrations, no message bus / streaming, no feature store, no model server, no OTel tracing, no graceful shutdown handler, no retry/timeout on the model call (the breaker swallows exceptions but there's no retry, no timeout), idempotency cache is in-memory and unbounded (memory leak + lost on restart), config has zero validation (no pydantic-settings, no env schema), audit log is local file only (single-replica, no replication).

   116→- Cross-referenced user's project tree (src/api/, src/rules/engine.py, src/features/, src/cases/service.py, dashboard/index.html, monitoring/grafana, docs/ARCHITECTURE_V3.md) to produce gap list
   117→- Appended this record to worklog.md
   118→
   119→Stage Summary:
   120→- Microsoft's reference is a 4-phase pipeline: **Ingest & process → Analyze, transform & enrich → Train & score → Visualize & activate**
   121→- Named components: Eventstreams (stream ingest) | Data Factory (batch ERP→lake sync) | Eventhouse (hot KQL streaming + raw tx table) | OneLake (cold governed lakehouse) | Data Science (ML workspace, online scoring) | Activator (declarative rules + auto-actions) | Real-Time Dashboard (live, DirectQuery, drill-down) | Power BI (BI/reporting, separate from ops dashboard) | Copilot (NL Q&A) | DirectQuery (live query mode)
   122→- Key sleekness drivers that make it look production-grade: (1) Real-Time Dashboard vs Power BI separation, (2) DirectQuery live-push mode, (3) declarative Activator rule routing w/ auto-blocking + auto-notifications, (4) governed OneLake lakehouse w/ raw→clean zones, (5) explicit hot (Eventhouse) vs cold (OneLake) split, (6) streaming transformations as first-class Eventhouse capability, (7) ERP batch path separated from transaction stream path by velocity, (8) Copilot NL layer, (9) RBAC/MFA/PAM/immutable-audit as named architectural sections, (10) capacity planning / autoscaling / lifecycle tiers as named sections, (11) phased rollout playbook (Foundation→Pilot→Ops Validation→Advanced→Enterprise-Scale)
   123→- Gap list vs user's RTO Trust Layer (concrete):
   124→  - User has rules engine but no Activator-equivalent declarative rule UI / auto-actioning layer (current: Python module + DEFAULT_RULES list, no rule DSL, no auto-block/auto-notify wiring beyond cases/service.py)
   125→  - User has ONE static dashboard/index.html "merchant console v2" — Microsoft splits into Real-Time Dashboard (live, DirectQuery, drill-down) + Power BI (reporting) + Copilot (NL). User needs: (a) live WebSocket/SSE push dashboard, (b) separate reporting dashboard, (c) LLM Q&A layer
   126→  - User has REST API ingest only (routes.py) — no Eventstream-equivalent streaming ingestion layer (Kafka/Redpanda/Redis Streams). ARCHITECTURE_V3 §9.3 already prescribes Redis Streams now → NATS → Kafka by trigger — implement that to match
   127→  - User has features/enrich.py called inline — Microsoft runs streaming transformations as a separate continuous stage. User needs a stream-processor step (Faust/Bytewax/Quix-like) for normalize/filter/aggregate
   128→  - User has cases/service.py (good parity) but unclear if there's a case queue UI; Microsoft's Activator orchestrates investigation workflows w/ SLA timers
   129→  - User has cost_table.md + cost_optimizer.py (actually AHEAD of Microsoft in explicit cost-model math) — surface this in architecture doc
   130→  - User has src/audit/logger.py + mandates.py + security.py (parity with Microsoft's RBAC/audit/MFA framing) — but the architecture doc needs to call these out as named architectural sections, not just modules
   131→  - User has src/models/explain.py — parity with Microsoft's "explainable AI for investigation support" — surface as first-class
   132→  - User has src/features/cleaning.py + enrich.py — needs explicit raw/clean/curated zone partitioning to match OneLake's governed lakehouse pattern (Iceberg/Delta tables via DuckDB+Parquet minimum)
   133→  - User has Prometheus + Grafana (parity with Microsoft's "system health dashboards" + "performance metrics w/ SLA monitoring") — Microsoft's observability is abstract; user's is concrete. User is actually ahead here.
   134→  - User has no ERP/asset enrichment batch path — Microsoft explicitly separates ERP batch sync (Data Factory → OneLake) from tx stream (Eventstreams → Eventhouse). Add a merchant-profile/device-registry batch ingest path
   135→  - User has ingest_kaggle.py (single dataset) — Microsoft explicitly designs for multi-channel (mobile banking + ATM + e-commerce + call center). User needs a multi-source ingestion story even if mocked
   136→  - ARCHITECTURE_V3.md is actually MORE rigorous than Microsoft's page (register-based decisions, revisit triggers, persona map) — but lacks a single named 4-phase architecture diagram. Redraw as Ingest→Analyze→Score→Activate.
   137→- Recommendation: User is NOT as far behind Microsoft as they fear. The "mid at best" feeling comes from (a) the single static HTML dashboard vs Microsoft's three-tier Real-Time Dashboard + Power BI + Copilot split, and (b) the absence of a streaming ingestion layer between producers and the API. Both are addressable with ~1 sprint of work, not a rewrite.

   300→- Step 1 read papers: paper studied/cost-sensitive-fraud-detection-bayes-minimum-risk/summary.md + skill.yaml (Bahnsen BMR, Eq.(5) cost matrix, Eq.(6) recalibration P*(f|x)=P(f|x)·P_orig/P_und); paper studied/cost-curves-classifier-performance/summary.md + skill.yaml (Drummond & Holte cost curves, bootstrap_performance_ci preserving row marginals, find_model_crossover, select_threshold_cost_minimally).
   301→- Step 2 read code: src/business/cost_optimizer.py (the existing optimal_decision fn — mathematically correct, NOT wired); src/api/routes.py (lines ~36 ACCEPT_T/REJECT_T static thresholds, lines ~141-208 decision pipeline, lines ~194-195 policy_hint field unused); tests/test_ship.py (cost-optimizer decision-theory check lines ~25-33); dashboard/index.html (hardcoded COSTS=[[0.15,1258],...] array line ~96, default demo keys visible line ~42-43). Also read mandates.py, breaker.py, security.py, metrics.py, audit/logger.py, registry.py, train.py, cleaning.py, enrich.py, rules/engine.py for context.
   302→- Step 3 wired optimal_decision: rewrote /risk/score decision block in routes.py. Precedence order: 1) Rules fast-path BLOCK → REJECT (rules_engine_block); 2a) Mandate BREACH → REJECT (mandate_breach); 2b) Mandate TAMPERED/EXPIRED with header → REJECT (mandate_invalid); 3) Circuit breaker OPEN or model failure → degraded rules-only REVIEW (degraded_review, degraded=true); 4) optimal_decision(p_rto, weights=DEFAULT_COST_WEIGHTS) → primary ACCEPT/REVIEW/REJECT (cost_optimal_bmr); REVIEW rule fires → never ACCEPT (cost_optimal_bmr_review_rule). Added new fields: decision_source (string), cost_breakdown (dict of per-decision expected costs). Kept legacy policy_hint for backward compat. Repurposed gate_thresholds response field to {"policy":"cost_optimal_bmr","weights":DEFAULT_COST_WEIGHTS,"legacy_accept_t":0.15,"legacy_reject_t":0.60}. Kept ACCEPT_T/REJECT_T as module-level legacy constants (used in gate_thresholds only — no longer consulted in decision path).
   303→- Step 4 added calibrate_probabilities: implemented in src/business/cost_optimizer.py. Bahnsen Eq.(6): P*(f|x) = P(f|x)·P_orig/P_und. No-op fast path when p_orig == p_und (also clips out-of-range inputs). Division-by-zero guard (p_und=0 → zeros). Handles scalar / list / tuple inputs, NaN propagation, [0,1] clipping. Docstring cites Bahnsen et al. ICMLA 2013, DOI 10.1109/ICMLA.2013.68.
   304→- Step 5 added /v1/policy/cost-curves endpoint: scorer scope, returns JSON with thresholds (0.05→0.95 step 0.05), curves (per-threshold {tp,fp,fn,tn,cost,precision,recall}), bootstrap_ci (≥500 row-marginal-preserving resamples, 90% level, low/high/mean per threshold), optimal_threshold (cost-minimizing), cost_crossover (single_model degenerate case for now — forward-compatible with Day 2 Track E model-registry), cost_model (with source_paper citations), data_source, n_samples, n_pos, n_neg. Added comment in routes.py: "# OpenAPI: this endpoint will appear in docs/openapi.json after next FastAPI app reload". Added DEFAULT_COST_WEIGHTS module-level dict (c_fp=50, c_fn=600, c_otp=5, c_block=1000, otp_effectiveness=0.82 — lockstep with optimal_decision() defaults). Added DEFAULT_COST_CURVE_RESAMPLES=500, DEFAULT_COST_CURVE_CONFIDENCE=0.90. Endpoint guards: 401 invalid scorer key, 422 n_resamples outside [1,5000] / confidence outside (0.5,0.999), 503 if model not loaded OR no labeled data. The endpoint never crashes — bootstrap is wrapped in try/except returning degenerate CIs on failure.
   305→- Added three helper functions in cost_optimizer.py: cost_curve_sweep(y_true, probs, c_fp, c_fn, thresholds) → list[{threshold,tp,fp,fn,tn,cost,precision,recall}]; bootstrap_cost_ci(y_true, probs, c_fp, c_fn, n_resamples, confidence, seed) → dict[str(threshold) → {low,high,mean,n_resamples,confidence}] (resamples within each class separately — preserves row marginals per Drummond-Holte §3.6); find_cost_crossover(incumbent, challenger) → {crossover_threshold, per_region_winner, max_advantage}. All pure-Python (no numpy dep) — keeps the BMR skill importable in minimal envs per skill.yaml dep spec.

   355→  - `cases`: case_id TEXT PK, prediction_id, order_id, merchant_id, status, priority, assigned_to, reason, created_at, updated_at, resolved_at, resolution_notes, resolution_by, resolution_decision. Indexes: ix_cases_status, ix_cases_prediction_id, ix_cases_created_at DESC.
   356→  - `model_registry`: version TEXT PK, model_path, metrics JSONB, is_champion BOOL, is_challenger BOOL, traffic_split FLOAT, drift_status TEXT, deployed_at, promoted_at. Indexes: UNIQUE PARTIAL ix_model_registry_single_champion ON (is_champion) WHERE is_champion = TRUE (enforces single-champion atomically), ix_model_registry_is_champion.
   357→  - `idempotency_keys`: key TEXT PK, request_body, response_body, status_code INT, created_at, expires_at. Index: ix_idempotency_keys_expires_at (for the 1% cleanup query).
   358→  - `psi_reference`: id SERIAL PK, feature_name, expected_distribution JSONB, n_bins INT, model_version, created_at. Index: ix_psi_reference_feature (feature_name, model_version).
   359→  All timestamps TIMESTAMPTZ (UTC-stable across worker restarts per RBI PA note). Downgrade reverse-order DROPs with IF EXISTS.
   360→- Step 5 audit/logger.py dual-mode: kept file-mode behaviour byte-for-byte (the byte-offset JSONL index + verify_chain + canonical() + redact_customer() + self_salt() now read via Settings.rto_audit_salt). Postgres mode: __init__ opens a persistent psycopg connection (single conn — no pool at this scale), `_hydrate_last_hash_postgres()` lazily SELECTs raw_hash FROM audit_records ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 1 to chain without a per-INSERT SELECT; `_log_postgres` computes audit_id=f"aud_{uuid4().hex[:16]}" (machine-friendly + URL-safe), INSERT into audit_records with mandate_type/bh_purpose_code/device_id/user_id as typed columns; `_read_postgres` SELECT body, raw_hash, prev_hash + merges hash fields into the body dict so file-mode test assertions work unchanged; `_verify_chain_postgres` SELECT audit_id, body, raw_hash, prev_hash ORDER BY id ASC, recomputes chain — math identical to file mode (canonical(body) + prev_hash). Added new `tail(limit)` method used by /v1/models/drift + audit-export CSV; refactored routes.py `_read_audit_tail` to call it so Postgres mode drift + audit-export actually return data (was broken in db mode — direct `audit_logger.path` read would return []).
   361→- Step 6 cases/service.py dual-mode: Postgres mode uses the separate `cases` table (one row per case — no event-merge step in list_cases; the file-mode OPENED/RESOLVED JSONL events are gone). `_open_postgres` INSERTs; `_resolve_postgres` UPDATEs SET status, resolution_decision, resolution_notes, resolution_by, resolved_at=NOW(), updated_at=NOW() WHERE case_id=%s (rowcount==0 → rollback + ValueError "case not found" → 422 at the API). `_list_postgres` SELECT * FROM cases WHERE status=%s OR %s IS NULL ORDER BY created_at DESC; datetime columns converted to ISO 8601 so the JSON-serializable contract holds across both modes. File mode unchanged.
   362→- Step 7 ml/registry.py dual-mode + lifespan wiring: Postgres mode `_register_model_postgres` uses UPSERT ON CONFLICT (version) DO UPDATE so re-registering the same version (e.g. lifespan re-registering v1234567890 after a worker restart) doesn't fail; atomically demotes prior champion (UPDATE ... WHERE is_champion=TRUE AND version<>%s) in the same transaction. `_current_champion_postgres` SELECT ... WHERE is_champion=TRUE ORDER BY deployed_at DESC, version DESC LIMIT 1. Added `_close_conn()` for shutdown. Module-level conn cache with `_conn_lock` (threadsafe). Wired into lifespan (Postgres mode only): `register_model(version=f"v{int(time.time())}", model_path=str(model_path), metrics={"pr_auc":..., "roc_auc":...}, champion=True)` — closes §A item 4 (register_model was dead in prod). File mode unchanged (the 63 tests don't trigger this; test_platform.py::test_registry_register_and_champion still passes via tmp_path). Lifespan shutdown calls `_close_registry_conn()` so workers don't leak Postgres connections across hot-reloads.
   363→- Step 8 idempotency TTLCache/Postgres: replaced `state["idem"] = {}` unbounded dict (§A item 2 leak) with TTLCache(maxsize=settings.idem_maxsize=10000, ttl=settings.idem_ttl_seconds=3600) in file mode. Postgres mode: cache lives in the `idempotency_keys` table; 3 module-level helpers in routes.py (`_idem_get_conn` lazily opens a dedicated psycopg connection cached on state; `_idem_lookup_postgres` SELECT response_body, status_code WHERE key=%s AND expires_at > NOW(); `_idem_store_postgres` INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (key) DO UPDATE so a slow retry that lands after the original INSERT refreshes expires_at rather than erroring; `_idem_cleanup_postgres` DELETE WHERE expires_at < NOW() called with 1% probability per request via `uuid.uuid4().int % 100 == 0`). File mode preserves the original (idempotency_key, order.model_dump_json()) tuple cache_key so two different request bodies with the same Idempotency-Key don't silently cross-pollute. Postgres mode uses just the header value as PK (the standard idempotency contract — same key, same response).

   388→Work Log:
   389→- Step 1 paper read: paper studied/tfx-production-scale-ml-platform/{summary.md,skill.yaml} — capability `generate_data_statistics` (distributed streaming approximation, HyperLogLog cited at scale). The TFX Data Analysis stage uses HyperLogLog for distinct-cardinality estimation across continuous streams since exact computation is infeasible; we port this pattern to Redis PFADD/PFCOUNT (Redis has built-in HyperLogLog). paper studied/mlops-devops-integration-scalable-ai-deployments/{summary.md,skill.yaml} — capability `mlops-devops-integration-planner` (plan_drift_triggered_retraining: "Define automated retraining triggers wired into the delivery pipeline (feedback loops from production), with monitoring hooks and retrain cadence policy"). Our model.drift stream → Track G's DDM/ADWIN detector is the production-side of that capability; the fire-and-forget publish is the "feedback loop" the MLOps paper calls out.
   390→- Step 2 code read: src/config.py (Track E — `Settings.redis_url: str | None = None` field already wired + documented as Track F Day 2; the `is_postgres` property filters on URL scheme; pattern matches the design I needed for StreamProducer's lazy-connect no-op contract); src/api/routes.py 1076 lines (Track C cost-optimizer decision section lines ~280-410; Track D mandate section lines ~167-280 + 326-425 + 760-830; Track E config block at lines ~120-145 + idempotency block at lines ~263-310 + 507-526 + 982-1090 helper functions — I added my publish calls + StreamProducer construction + the prediction_id extraction in the decision section ONLY, additive; the 3 Track E owned sections are untouched); docker-compose.yml (Track E moved postgres out of `["full"]` profile; redis still profile-gated — I moved redis out too); requirements.txt + pyproject.toml (Track E added 4 deps; I added 1 more: redis>=5.0).

   392→- Step 4 src/stream/ package (NEW):
   393→  - `src/stream/__init__.py` — re-exports the 5 stream-name constants + StreamProducer + StreamConsumer + run_consumer so `from src.stream import StreamProducer` works. Documents the design principle (fire-and-forget, NOT the full transactional outbox per V3 §10.3 — deferred enhancement) + the V2 §5 5-stream inventory + the decision rationale (Redis Streams over Kafka per 04-TECH-STACK-DECISIONS).
   394→  - `src/stream/producer.py` — `StreamProducer` class. `__init__(redis_url)` stores the URL + sets `client=None` + `_connect_attempted=False` (lazy connect — the 63 existing tests import routes.py which imports StreamProducer; without lazy connect every test would import-time-fail when redis-py isn't installed). `_ensure_client()` is idempotent (one attempt; never retries on every publish — if Redis was down at first publish, the worker is typically down for the duration). `publish(stream, fields)` coerces ALL field values to str (Redis Streams XADD requires bytes/str; a non-str scalar like `float(p_rto)` would raise `DataError` — the producer coerces + never raises; the API caller doesn't see the publish outcome by design). On any exception (Redis down, network blip, OOM on Redis side), prints to stderr + returns None. `close()` is safe to call multiple times + clears the `_connect_attempted` flag so a re-init can re-attempt.
   395→  - `src/stream/consumer.py` — `StreamConsumer` class + `run_consumer()` entrypoint. `XGROUP CREATE` is idempotent (MKSTREAM + BUSYGROUP swallowed — can't import the typed exception since redis-py isn't in the sandbox, so I match on the class name string). `XREADGROUP >` reads only new messages (count=10, block=5000ms — 5s default is a good balance between immediate pickup + low idle CPU). On handler exception, the message is NOT acked (stays PEL for XCLAIM re-delivery — full claim loop deferred per spec). Installs SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers so `docker compose stop` cleanly drains (doesn't leave a half-acked PEL entry). `_default_handler` logs to stderr in `key=value` grep-friendly format so `docker compose logs stream-worker` shows the event flow end-to-end. `run_consumer()` is the entrypoint for `python -m src.stream.consumer` — drains risk.scores + audit.records + cases.created (the three streams the API publishes to with immediate consumer use-case; model.drift is drained by processor.py via its own group; notifications is reserved for Track H/I to install a custom handler).
   396→  - `src/stream/processor.py` — `StreamProcessor` class + `run_processor()` entrypoint. This is the Microsoft Eventhouse streaming-transforms equivalent (closes §D item P7). Consumes risk.scores via its OWN consumer group `rto-processors` (so the stream-worker above doesn't compete for messages — Redis Streams supports multiple groups per stream, each sees all messages). Implements the TFX `generate_data_statistics` pattern: Redis `PFADD`/`PFCOUNT` for HyperLogLog cardinality of distinct order_ids per minute bucket (survives process restarts; ~0.81% error at 1B elements, well within drift-detection tolerance); in-memory `deque[(ts, score)]` for sliding-window velocity + rolling mean/std. 3 anomaly detectors: `duplicate_order_id` (same order_id published twice within the window — strong RTO signal), `score_velocity_spike` (message rate > 3x rolling baseline), `score_mean_drift` (rolling mean > 2 sigma from baseline — streaming-PSI equivalent). On anomaly, publishes to `model.drift` with structured `anomaly_reason` field so Track G's DDM/ADWIN detector can route each reason to the right handler (retrain PR vs alert only vs webhook to merchant). Geographic impossibility detection is a documented placeholder (the published fields don't carry geo — only logged).

   415→- DEFERRED enhancement (V3 §10.3 full transactional outbox): the fire-and-forget publish is the pragmatic hackathon pattern. The full pattern is: API writes audit row + outbox row in the SAME Postgres transaction (atomic); a separate outbox-drainer worker reads the outbox table + XADDs to Redis + DELETEs the outbox row on success. This guarantees at-least-once delivery even if Redis is down at publish time. For the hackathon demo, the current fire-and-forget is fine — Redis is local + the producer tolerates Redis-down silently. Note in worklog that the outbox table schema would live in alembic/versions/002_outbox.py (Track E Day 2 created 001_initial.py with 5 tables; Track H or a future Track would add the outbox table).
   416→- DEFERRED enhancement (XCLAIM re-delivery loop): when a consumer's handler raises, the message stays in the PEL (Pending Entries List). The current consumer doesn't run an XCLAIM loop to redeliver stuck-PEL messages to other consumers after a timeout. For the hackathon demo this is fine — the workers are `restart: unless-stopped` so a crashed worker restarts + the next handler invocation succeeds. Full XCLAIM loop would add: every N seconds, scan PEL for messages older than T, XCLAIM them to this consumer, re-invoke handler. Track M Day 4 could add this if needed.

   423→Agent: general-purpose (Track G — Feedback loop + DDM/ADWIN drift detection)

   425→
   426→Work Log:
   427→- Step 1 paper read: `paper studied/survey-concept-drift-adaptation/{summary.md,skill.yaml}` — Gama, Žliobaitė, Bifet, Pechenizkiy, Bouchachia 2014. Key formulas ported: DDM (§3.2) — `p_i` running error rate, `σ_i = √(p·(1-p)/i)` Bernoulli std, baseline `(p_min, σ_min)` updated on strictly-tighter `(p+σ)`, WARNING at `p+σ ≥ p_min + 2·σ_min` (95%), DRIFT at `p+σ ≥ p_min + 3·σ_min` (99%); ADWIN (§3.3, Bifet & Gavalda 2007) — variable-length sliding window, Hoeffding bound `ε_cut = √((1/2m)·ln(4|W|/δ))`, δ=0.002 default. Survey Table II: DDM = O(1)/O(1), ADWIN = O(log W)/O(W) (deque variant here is O(W) — fine at 10k cap). §5 detector-quality metrics (false-alarm run length, detection delay) wired as Prometheus summaries. §6 explicitly names fraud/RTO scorers as the "Monitoring and Control" application category — chargeback-style delayed feedback = the survey's "credit-scoring 1-year horizon" pattern. skill.yaml capability `wrap_model_with_drift_detector` lands in `LabelFeedbackService`; `plan_adaptation_strategy`'s retrain-trigger half is the `_trigger_shadow_retrain` notification publish.
   428→- Step 2 code read: `src/stream/{producer,consumer,processor}.py` (Track F) — StreamProducer lazy-connect fire-and-forget contract (publish returns None silently if Redis down OR REDIS_URL unset, never raises); StreamConsumer XREADGROUP consumer-group semantics + XACK on success + signal handlers; StreamProcessor consumes risk.scores via group `rto-processors` + publishes anomalies to `model.drift` with `anomaly_reason ∈ {duplicate_order_id, score_velocity_spike, score_mean_drift}`. The 5 stream-name constants live in `src/stream/producer.py` — I imported them rather than re-declaring. `src/ml/registry.py` (Track E) — has `psi()` batch distribution-drift + `model_registry.drift_status` column (init "unknown" — Track G is the formal-detector complement, the column-update wire is a deferred enhancement). `src/api/routes.py` (full 1158 lines) — Track C cost-optimizer decision section lines ~280-410, Track D mandate section ~167-280 + 326-425 + 760-830, Track E config block ~120-145 + idempotency block ~263-310 + 507-526 + 982-1090 helpers, Track F publish calls ~481-517. `src/api/metrics.py` — simple Metrics class with counters + gauges + a single named latency summary; needed extending for arbitrary named summaries (Track G §5 detector-quality metrics). `src/api/security.py` — `bearer_token` + `check_key` pattern; admin-scope enforcement for the feedback endpoint mirrors the case-resolve + override pattern (403, not 401, for scope-vs-auth).
   429→- Step 3 src/ml/drift.py (NEW, 244 lines): pure-Python + `math` + `collections.deque` (no new deps — numpy only used in `psi()` batch). `class DDM` — survey §3.2 implementation. The well-known DDM degeneracy guard (when the model is perfect, p collapses to 0 → σ_min collapses to 0 → 3σ threshold becomes 0 → every sample fires DRIFT) is fixed by gating the baseline update on `σ > 0` AND gating the WARNING/DRIFT fire on `σ_min > 0` (standard MOA-style fix — documented in the docstring with the degeneracy explanation). Survey §3.2's `p_min` is `p` at the minimum point (NOT `p+σ` — the survey stores p_min and σ_min separately, so `p_min + σ_min` IS the (p+σ) value at the minimum point). Initial bug fix: I first stored `p_min = p+σ`; the test `test_ddm_warning_before_drift` failed (all STABLE on a 100% error burst) because the baseline kept "chasing" the rising error rate. Fixed by storing `p_min = p` per the survey's exact spec. `reset()` called after a confirmed DRIFT (the survey §4 recommendation: re-establish the baseline from the new concept). `class ADWIN` — survey §3.3 + Bifet & Gavalda 2007. `deque(maxlen=10_000)` for bounded memory. Midpoint cut check (simpler than the full all-cut-points ADWIN — survey §3.3 ablation says midpoint is ~99% as effective). On cut, keeps only the second (more recent) half — drops the older concept. Marginal-cut = WARNING (sub-threshold shift), strong-cut = DRIFT (>2·ε_cut). `detect_drift_stream()` batch helper for replay / prequential evaluation (Gama 2014 §5).
   430→- Step 4 src/feedback/ package (NEW):
   431→  - `src/feedback/__init__.py` — re-exports `LabelFeedbackService` + `run_drift_consumer`.
   432→  - `src/feedback/label_service.py` (336 lines) — `LabelFeedbackService`. Constructed once at API boot (`state["feedback"]`) so in-memory DDM/ADWIN state persists across requests within one worker. `ingest_label(prediction_id, is_returned, predicted_p, threshold=0.15)` — computes the Bernoulli error indicator (XOR of "predicted RTO?" vs "actually returned?"), updates both detectors, fires `_trigger_shadow_retrain` on DRIFT, resets detectors post-DRIFT. `DEFAULT_RETURN_THRESHOLD = 0.15` matches the cost-optimizer's legacy `ACCEPT_T` so the error indicator is semantically consistent with the decision layer. `consume_anomaly(anomaly_reason, prediction_id)` — the anomaly-side path (drains `model.drift` via `src/feedback/drift_consumer`). Tracks in-memory run-length of consecutive same-reason anomalies (resets on a different reason — one-off `score_velocity_spike` followed by `score_mean_drift` shouldn't fire a retrain). When run-length hits `DRIFT_SIGNAL_RUN_LENGTH=3`, fires a parallel `retrain_request` (the fast-reactive path: streaming PSI catches shifts within seconds vs days for the label-side DDM). `_trigger_shadow_retrain()` publishes to the `notifications` stream via the lazy StreamProducer (Track F's contract: no-op when Redis down). `current_state()` snapshot for the /metrics endpoint gauges. Threadsafe (`threading.Lock` around the update path). Multi-process safety caveat documented (in-memory state is per-worker — full Redis-shared detector state is a deferred enhancement; single-worker deployment is fine for the demo).
   433→  - `src/feedback/drift_consumer.py` (96 lines) — `run_drift_consumer()` entrypoint + `_make_handler(service)` builder. Constructs `StreamConsumer` joined to the **3rd consumer group `rto-drift-detectors`** on the `model.drift` stream (the first two are `rto-workers` from Track F's stream-worker + `rto-processors` from stream-processor). Each `model.drift` message routes to `LabelFeedbackService.consume_anomaly` for the run-length heuristic + retrain-trigger fire. REDIS_URL-unset guard exits cleanly with a stderr message (same pattern as Track F's `run_consumer`).

   451→- Closes: §A item 18 (feedback loop — the `model.drift` stream produced by Track F's stream-processor now has a consumer; the delayed is_returned labels flow into DDM/ADWIN via /v1/feedback/ingest; on DRIFT the retrain_request notification fires); §D P3 (formal drift detection — DDM 2σ/3σ + ADWIN Hoeffding bound, both per the source paper); §D P4 (shadow-retrain trigger — the notifications stream publish is the trigger; Track H installs the actual PR-opening handler); driver G3 partial (the formal detectors land here; the PSI reference distribution population is deferred — Track E created the psi_reference table, Track G hasn't populated it); MLOps-DevOps paper's `plan_drift_triggered_retraining` capability is fully closed (the prod-side retrain trigger + monitoring hooks + retrain cadence policy all wired).    452→- DDM degeneracy-guard detail: when the model is perfectly correct on the seed stream, p collapses to 0, σ_min collapses to 0, and the 3σ threshold becomes 0 → every subsequent sample trivially fires DRIFT. Fixed by gating baseline update on σ > 0 + gating WARNING/DRIFT fire on σ_min > 0 (MOA-style fix, documented in the docstring). Discovered + fixed during test-driven iteration: my initial code stored `p_min = p+σ` (treating p_min as the minimum of the (p+σ) series), but the survey §3.2 stores `p_min = p` (the error rate at the minimum point, with σ_min separately tracking the std at that point — so `p_min + σ_min` IS the (p+σ) at the minimum). The first form caused the baseline to keep "chasing" a rising error rate (baseline updated whenever current (p+σ) < previous (p+σ)+previous σ_min — too loose). The corrected form (`p_min = p`) made the test_ddm_warning_before_drift test pass: WARNING fires at p+σ ≈ 0.265 + DRIFT at p+σ ≈ 0.319 on the 100%-error-burst scenario.

DRIFT`. Also: ingest delayed labels via POST /v1/feedback/ingest for a sustained error burst → DDM fires DRIFT → retrain_request fires → /metrics shows rto_drift_ddm_state=2 + Grafana panel 5 turns red.
   454→- DEFERRED to Track H (Day 2, V3 missing endpoints + notifications): the `notifications` stream is consumed by Track G's `_trigger_shadow_retrain` (publish side) but Track H installs the consumer side (GitHub webhook → actually open a retraining PR; or trigger the shadow-retrain pipeline).    455→- DEFERRED to Track H (Day 2, audit-body index): the feedback endpoint scans the last 5000 audit records to find the prediction_id match (file mode = JSONL tail; Postgres mode = SELECT body FROM audit_records ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 5000). At scale, a `jsonb_path_query` index on `audit_records.body->prediction_id` would replace the scan — a Track H V3 §10.3 work item.    456→- DEFERRED to Track M (Day 4, OpenTelemetry): the LabelFeedbackService's DDM/ADWIN update path + the drift-consumer's per-message handler are not wrapped in OTel spans. Track M can add spans for the detector update + retrain-trigger publish if distributed tracing is needed.
   457→- DEFERRED enhancement (multi-process shared detector state): in a multi-worker deployment (uvicorn --workers N), each worker's LabelFeedbackService sees a different slice of the label stream — the in-memory DDM/ADWIN state is partial. The robust fix is to share detector state via Redis (a single `rto:drift:ddm` hash updated atomically per label via HINCRBY + a Lua script for the σ_min comparison). Single-worker deployment is fine for the demo. Documented in label_service.py docstring.
   458→- DEFERRED enhancement (ADWIN O(log W) variant): the survey §3.3 + Bifet & Gavalda 2007 specify an exponential-histogram data structure for O(log W) memory. This implementation uses a `deque(maxlen=10_000)` for O(W) memory — fine at our scale (10k cap per worker), but the exponential variant would scale to 1M+ samples. A future Track could swap in the histogram if needed.

   476→- Step 3 migration: NEW `alembic/versions/002_merkle_intervals.py` (revision 002, down_revision 001). Additive — Track E's `001_initial.py` is intact. Creates `audit_merkle_intervals` table (interval_id SERIAL PK, start/end_record_id BIGINT, merkle_root TEXT, prev_interval_root TEXT — chains intervals together same as per-record prev_hash chains records, leaf_count INT, sealed_at TIMESTAMPTZ) + 2 indexes (sealed_at, merkle_root). Adds `audit_records.interval_id INT REFERENCES audit_merkle_intervals(interval_id)` + `audit_records.interval_position INT` columns (IF NOT EXISTS so idempotent) + composite index `(interval_id, interval_position)` for the proof-builder query. Downgrade reverse-order: DROP INDEX → DROP COLUMNs → DROP TABLE.
   477→- Step 4 MerkleSealer (NEW, in `src/audit/logger.py`, ~280 lines added): `class MerkleSealer` with `add(record_id, raw_hash)` / `seal()` / `proof(record_id)` / `_merkle_root(leaves)` (static). Sealing triggers: count-based (default 1000 records) OR time-based (default 3600s since interval opened) — whichever trips first. Padding rule RFC 6962-style: pad leaves to next power of 2 by repeating the LAST leaf's hash (no synthetic zero-leaf that a verifier would need to special-case). Empty leaves → GENESIS `"0"*64` (same constant as Track E's per-record chain genesis). Proof builder: walks the tree from leaf to root, recording sibling hash + position ("left"/"right") at each level; pre-fetches all leaves in the interval for O(M) query cost rather than one query per tree level.
   478→- Step 5 AuditLogger wiring (Track H adds ON TOP of Track E's dual-mode — file mode unchanged): `__init__` constructs `self.sealer = MerkleSealer(conn=self._conn)` in Postgres mode (None in file mode); `_log_postgres` adds `RETURNING id` to the INSERT, captures the new record's SERIAL id, calls `self.sealer.add(record_id, raw_hash)` after commit (best-effort try/except — Merkle sealing must NEVER break the audit write; the per-record hash chain is the foundation + Merkle is a coarser layer that can catch up via `seal_interval()` later). Added 4 new AuditLogger public methods: `seal_interval()` (cron/shutdown hook), `merkle_proof(record_id)`, `merkle_intervals(limit=100)` (admin/compliance listing), `usage_counts(since_hours=(24,168,720))` (Postgres index-only scan + file-mode JSONL timestamp scan). File mode keeps the per-record hash chain only — Merkle is a Postgres-mode enhancement (sufficient for tests + local dev).

   534→- Step 4 mlops.yml: NEW `.github/workflows/mlops.yml`. 7 stages map to TFX Baylor 2017 §3 + CD + Monitor stages from Paleyes 2022 + MLOps-DevOps Integration:
   535→  - Stage 1 `data-analysis` — TFX `generate_data_statistics`; runs `scripts/profile_data.py` (pandas.describe + value_counts fallback; whylogs optional lazy-import); uploads out/data_profile.html + out/data_profile.json as artifact `data-profile` (retention 30d).
   536→  - Stage 2 `data-validation` — TFX `build_and_apply_schema`; runs `scripts/validate_data.py` (manual schema checks for required columns, dtype drift, null > 50% hard floor, categorical allowed-set enforcement for PaymentMethod + DeliveryStatus + warn_on_new_level for CityTier + Category, numeric range checks, regex format checks via re.search not re.match); exits 1 on blocking anomalies.
   537→  - Stage 3 `model-training` — TFX `Trainer` + warm-starting; postgres:15-alpine service container; `alembic upgrade head`; `python scripts/evaluate.py --feature-set full --out out/metrics.json` (existing Track A script — produces out/model.joblib + out/metrics.json); hard PR-AUC >= 0.60 gate via inline Python heredoc reading out/metrics.json (Kandula 2021 benchmark AUC 0.73-0.79 — anything below 0.60 is worse than random on the positive class); register_model(version=f"ci-{GITHUB_SHA[:12]}", model_path="out/model.joblib", metrics={pr_auc, roc_auc, threshold_best_f1, confusion}, champion=True) via inline Python heredoc (Track E Postgres-backed registry makes the demote-on-promote atomic); uploads `model-artifact` containing out/model.joblib + out/metrics.json (retention 90d for model lineage traceability).
   538→  - Stage 4 `model-gate` — TFX `gate_model_promotion`; postgres:15-alpine service; `alembic upgrade head`; downloads `model-artifact` to out/; `python scripts/canary_gate.py --model out/model.joblib --data data/raw/cod_orders.csv --max-regression 0.05` (compares new model PR-AUC + cost-weighted error against previous champion from registry; absolute floor 0.60; regression ceiling 5% vs incumbent; canary trivially passes if no incumbent — first CI run only); `python scripts/slice_metrics.py --model out/model.joblib --data data/raw/cod_orders.csv --out out/slice_metrics.json` (per-slice PR-AUC for merchant_category / cod_vs_prepaid / pin_code_tier; TFX small-slice warning — flag if aggregate improves but slice degrades > 10%); uploads `slice-metrics` artifact.
   539→  - Stage 5 `container-build` — CD container build; setup-buildx-action@v3; docker/login-action@v3 (registry=ghcr.io, username=github.actor, password=secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN); docker/build-push-action@v5 (push=true, tags=ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}:${{ github.sha }} + ghcr.io/${{ github.repository }}:ci-latest, cache-from=type=gha + cache-to=type=gha,mode=max); permissions: contents=read + packages:write.
   540→  - Stage 6 `deploy-staging` — CD deploy; needs container-build; `if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'` (PRs only run stages 1-5; canary gate passes/fails but no staging deploy); environment: staging + url https://staging.rto-trust-layer.example.com; placeholder deploy step (echo — Track M Day 4 wires real kubectl/helm); grafana/k6-action@v0.3.1 against tests/load/risk_api_load.js with env BASE_URL=secrets.STAGING_URL + SCORER_KEY=secrets.STAGING_SCORER_KEY.
   541→  - Stage 7 `monitor` — CD monitor; needs deploy-staging; env PROMETHEUS_URL=secrets.STAGING_PROMETHEUS_URL + ERROR_RATE_THRESHOLD=0.01 (matches k6 http_req_failed: rate<0.01); `pip install requests` (minimal monitor env — no pandas/sklearn needed); `python scripts/check_error_rate.py --prom-url "$PROMETHEUS_URL" --threshold "$ERROR_RATE_THRESHOLD"` (queries Prometheus `sum(rate(risk_decisions_total{decision="REJECT"}[5m])) / sum(rate(risk_decisions_total[5m]))`; tolerant of missing Prometheus for Buildathon demo; `--require` flag for real production deploys); rollback step `if: failure()` (echo — Track M Day 4 wires real kubectl rollout undo).

   545→  - `scripts/validate_data.py` — TFX stage 2; EXPECTED_COLUMNS dict (16 cols: OrderID/CustomerID/OrderDay/CityTier/State/Category/OrderValue/DiscountPct/PaymentMethod/Device/AddressQuality/OrderHour/Items/PriorOrders/PriorReturns/DeliveryStatus) with per-col dtype + max_null_pct + allowed set + warn_on_new_level + min/max numeric range + regex format check; HARD_NULL_FLOOR_PCT=50 (block on unusable data); try_pandera(df) optional lazy-import (returns (False, []) if not installed); validate(df, strict=False) → (blocking_errors, warnings); BLOCKING vs WARNING semantics (TFX actionable anomaly descriptions); `re.search` not `re.match` for regex (handles "Rs.866"/"INR 10,579" prefixes); `--strict` flag for hard mode.    546→  - `scripts/canary_gate.py` — TFX stage 4; cost_weighted_error(y_true, y_pred, fp_cost=1.0, fn_cost=12.0) per Bahnsen 2013 + scripts/cost_table.py defaults; evaluate_model(model, X, y, thr=0.5) → {pr_auc, cost_weighted_error, threshold}; get_incumbent_metrics() → fetches previous champion from src.ml.registry (current_champion() + load_registry() file-mode fallback for the demoted entry); compare(canary_metrics, incumbent_metrics, max_regression) → absolute floor 0.60 + regression ceiling 5% vs incumbent on PR-AUC (lower=worse) + cost-weighted error (higher=worse); writes out/canary_metrics.json. Smoke-tested on the trained model (PR-AUC 0.55): exits 1 with "canary PR-AUC 0.5495 below absolute floor 0.6 — model NOT promotable" — the gate correctly blocks the underperforming canary.
   547→  - `scripts/slice_metrics.py` — TFX stage 4 cont.; SLICES list (merchant_category on `Category` col, cod_vs_prepaid on `is_cod` col, pin_code_tier on `city_tier` col); MIN_SLICE_SIZE=30 (slices below this are "too_small" not regressed); DEFAULT_MAX_SLICE_REGRESSION=0.10 (looser than canary gate's 5% because per-slice metrics are noisier — TFX recommended pattern "loose slice gate + tight aggregate gate"); evaluate_slices(model, X, y, df) → per-slice PR-AUC + delta_vs_aggregate + status (ok/regressed/too_small); TFX small-slice warning when aggregate improves but slice degrades. Smoke-tested: 5 slices regressed (Electronics, Home & Kitchen, prepaid, tier_1, tier_2) — exit 1 with detailed per-slice table.
   548→  - `scripts/check_error_rate.py` — TFX stage 7; QUERY = `sum(rate(risk_decisions_total{decision="REJECT"}[5m])) / sum(rate(risk_decisions_total[5m]))`; query_prometheus(prom_url, query, timeout=5.0) → float or None (tolerant of network errors); DEFAULT_REQUIRED_FAILURES=3 (consecutive failures before trip — prevents flapping on a single 5m spike); DEFAULT_EVAL_INTERVAL_S=60 (60s × 3 = 3 min sustained error before rollback); `--once` flag for CI mode (GitHub Actions runner can't sleep 5 min between polls); `--require` flag for real production (fail hard if Prometheus unreachable); tolerates missing Prometheus for Buildathon demo (returns 0 with notice "Prometheus returned no data — assuming no traffic yet"). Smoke-tested: with no Prometheus → exit 0 (notice mode); with `--require` → exit 1 (error mode).

   658→- Step 2 added optimal_intervention(): src/business/cost_optimizer.py. INTERVENTIONS = ("ship", "otp_verify", "partial_cod", "address_check", "hold"); DEFAULT_INTERVENTION_WEIGHTS dict with c_otp=5 + eff_otp=0.82, c_partial_cod=10 + eff=0.65, c_address_check=3 + eff=0.45, c_hold=20 + residual_ship_rate=0.30, c_block=1000. Function computes per-intervention expected cost using per-amount FN cost (Bahnsen Eq.(5)): ship=p*amt, otp_verify=c_otp+(1-eff)*p*amt, partial_cod=c_partial_cod+(1-eff)*p*amt, address_check=c_address_check+(1-eff)*p*amt, hold=c_hold+residual*p*amt. Returns (intervention, costs). Docstring cites Bahnsen 2013 BMR + Pragma 2025 effectiveness rates.
   659→- Step 3 added per-amount FN cost flag: optimal_decision() now accepts amount_inr: float | None = None. When provided, the FN cost = amount_inr (Bahnsen Eq.(5) — NOT the constant c_fn=600). When None, falls back to c_fn (Track C's behavior, backward compat). Docstring updated with the per-amount math + the Bahnsen 2013 Table III reference.
   660→- Step 4 wired optimal_intervention() into routes.py decision section: after the 3-way optimal_decision() call (which now keeps Track C's constant-c_fn=600 default — NOT passing amount_inr, to preserve Track C's tests), added a parallel optimal_intervention(proba, order.amount_inr) call.    661→- Important design note: the 3-way optimal_decision() call in routes.py KEEPS the constant c_fn=600 (Track C's behavior). The per-amount FN cost (Bahnsen Eq.(5)) is wired ONLY into the 5-way optimal_intervention() call. Rationale: Track C's existing tests assert body["cost_breakdown"][action] == optimal_decision(body["probability"])[1][action] WITHOUT amount_inr — passing amount_inr would change the breakdown and break Track C's tests. The 3-way decision is the primary authorization signal (backward compat); the 5-way intervention is the operator's next-step recommendation (Track N's new surface). This is consistent with the task spec Step 3 (just add the flag) + Step 4 (wire optimal_intervention, not change the existing optimal_decision call).

   671→- Cost-model property documented: with DEFAULT weights (Pragma 2025 effectiveness rates), otp_verify dominates the "soft" interventions for any p*amt ≥ 6 INR because c_otp=5 is cheap AND eff_otp=0.82 is the highest effectiveness. The ship→otp_verify crossover is at p*amt ≈ c_otp/eff_otp = 5/0.82 ≈ 6.10 INR. partial_cod/address_check/hold become optimal only when their weights are re-tuned (e.g. higher c_otp in a market without cheap SMS gateways, or lower c_hold + residual_ship_rate when manual review is cheap + blocks 99% of held orders). The tests verify both the default-weight behavior AND the re-tuned crossover so the cost-model's truthful answer is documented — the argmin is a function of (p, amt, weights), not a constant.

The endpoint surfaces the data; the visualization is a frontend concern. (b) Per-call intervention weights override via an /risk/score query param or header — currently the weights are fixed to DEFAULT_INTERVENTION_WEIGHTS at the module level; an operator who wants to override (e.g. c_otp=25 for an IVR-only market) must edit the module-level dict. A future Track can add a /v1/policy/intervention-weights admin endpoint + a per-call override header. (c) The 3-way optimal_decision() call in routes.py still uses the constant c_fn=600 (Track C's behavior, backward compat) — the per-amount FN cost is wired only into the 5-way optimal_intervention() call. A future Track can flip the 3-way call to also pass amount_inr=order.amount_inr (the per-amount FN cost) once Track C's tests are updated to assert the per-amount breakdown. (d) The intervention is a recommendation, not enforced — the operator can execute a different intervention (e.g. hold even when otp_verify is cost-optimal). The audit hash chain captures the recommendation vs the executed decision (the BMR-vs-execution gap); a future Track can add an alerting rule that flags when the operator's executed intervention diverges from the cost-optimizer's recommendation by more than X% of the optimal cost.

   690→- Step 4 new `scripts/retrain_real.py` (~270 lines): full real-data retrain pipeline per spec. Flow: (1) read `data/raw/ingested_real.csv` via `load_ingested_real`; (2) `group_split(df)` GroupShuffleSplit on CustomerID test_size=0.2 random_state=42; (3) `assert leakage == 0` CI gate (group_leakage assertion fails loudly if any customer leaked across train/test — breaks every paper-cited comparison); (4) `build_feature_frame(train_df, "order+addr")` + `fit_model(X_tr, y_tr)` (HistGB, unchanged from synthetic path); (5) evaluate PR-AUC + ROC-AUC + F1 + precision/recall@F1-best-threshold (same `best_f1_threshold` logic as evaluate.py); (6) `current_champion()` to read the incumbent's metrics (Postgres-backed per Track E; falls back to `out/model_registry.json` in file mode); (7) `register_model(version=f"real-{int(time.time())}", model_path="out/model_real.joblib", metrics={...}, champion=True)` if new PR-AUC > incumbent's (or `--promote-always`); (8) write `out/metrics_real.json` (same shape as `out/metrics.json` so the dashboard's Model Health page reads either); (9) shell out to `scripts/cost_table.py --data data/raw/ingested_real.csv --out docs/cost_table.md` (subprocess — the `load_orders` dispatch in cleaning.py auto-routes to `load_ingested_real` for the unified-schema CSV); (10) regenerate `docs/feature_importance.md` via `src.models.explain.global_importance` (permutation AP-drop, n_repeats=10, scoring="average_precision"); (11) print summary "Retrained on N real orders. PR-AUC: X.XX (was Y.YY on synthetic). Champion: real-{version}."; (12) exit 1 if PR-AUC < 0.60 (CI gate per `mlops.yml` Stage 3 — the Kandula 2021 benchmark is AUC 0.73-0.79; PR-AUC < 0.60 means the model is worse than random on the positive class — block promotion + fail the pipeline). CLI flags: `--data`, `--feature-set`, `--model-out`, `--metrics-out`, `--no-cost-table`, `--no-feature-importance`, `--promote-always`.

   702→- POTENTIAL ISSUE for user (Kaggle download): the Amazon India Sale Report dataset on Kaggle may have a slightly different schema than the column map I guessed at. The `data/raw/README.md` troubleshooting table covers the most likely divergences (`Order ID` vs `order_id`, `ship-state` vs `ship_state`, etc.) and the user can update `COLUMN_MAPS["amazon"]` in `scripts/ingest_kaggle.py` if a column name is different. The `[warn] no source for '<column>'` message printed to stderr during ingestion flags any missing column. The unified-schema CSV is what `load_ingested_real` reads — if a column is missing it's filled with pd.NA + a sensible default in `load_ingested_real` (e.g. `device="unknown"`, `address_quality="unknown"`, `discount_pct=0.0`).
   703→- POTENTIAL ISSUE for user (is_cod inference): the Amazon India Sale Report has no explicit `payment_method` column, so `is_cod` is inferred from the `B2B` flag (B2B → prepaid → is_cod=0; B2C → COD → is_cod=1). This is a coarse heuristic — B2C orders CAN be prepaid (e.g. UPI payment at checkout). On real Amazon data this may cause `is_cod` to overestimate the COD share. The user can override `infer_is_cod()` in `scripts/ingest_kaggle.py` if a better signal is available (e.g. if a future Kaggle release adds a `payment_method` column, the existing code already handles it via the `payment_method` candidate in the column map).
   704→- POTENTIAL ISSUE for user (CustomerID proxy): the Amazon India Sale Report has no customer identifier, so `load_ingested_real` synthesises `CustomerID = ship-state|ship-postal-code` as a coarse proxy. This means `PriorOrders` + `PriorReturns` are computed per (state, pincode) pair, not per real customer. The Kandula 2021 paper's `predict_delivery_success_profile` capability expects a real customer key. Track N's Feast feature store will refine this once Day 4 lands — the unified-schema CSV carries `order_id` so a real `CustomerID` could be backfilled from a customer-lookup table in a future Track. The current proxy is conservative (it gives the model a meaningful prior-orders signal on real data instead of the degenerate "all new customers" baseline) and the `group_split` leakage-safe split still works correctly on the proxy.

Truncating `out/audit.jsonl` + `out/cases.jsonl` before each test run is the workaround. Verified by reverting `src/features/cleaning.py` to its original 47-line state and re-running: same flake. The file-based audit logger (Track E dual-mode file fallback) doesn't isolate per-test.    796→## Honest verdict
   797→
   798→- **REAL: 6 of 8** (Claims 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7)
   799→- **DECORATIVE: 2 of 8** (Claims 1 and partially 8)
   800→- **STUB: 0**
   801→- **MISSING: 0**
   802→
   803→The mandate engine itself (mandates.py + audit wiring + tests) is genuinely well-built and the prior session's Track D claims are substantively accurate for the verify_mandate() / OC-201B cap-enforcement story. The BoundedAgent class, however, is a demo-script-only artifact — it is NOT in the production code path (production routes.py uses verify_mandate() directly), and no pytest asserts its dispatch behavior. The 7-action allowlist is real code but its enforcement is decorative: the only thing that actually runs in production is the X-Mandate + X-Device-Id + X-User-Id header path through verify_mandate, which the tests cover thoroughly.
   804→
   805→Also flagging: prior session claimed "63/63 total tests pass" — actual count is **117 passed + 8 skipped** in 37.46s. The project has grown well beyond 63 tests; the worklog entry is stale.
   806→
   807→## Specific gaps for orchestrator
   808→
   809→1. **scripts/demo_agent.py:72-147 (BoundedAgent class) — has no test coverage.** Add `tests/test_bounded_agent.py` that imports BoundedAgent, asserts dispatch("refund_order") → "Action not permitted", dispatch("upi_circle_delegated_pay", amount_inr=6000) → "exceeds per-txn cap", dispatch("upi_circle_delegated_pay", amount_inr=3000) → "I cannot perform this action. I have requested human approval." Right now the 7-action allowlist is a doc claim — the only thing that exercises it is a manual `python scripts/demo_agent.py` run.
   810→
   811→2. **scripts/demo_agent.py:72 BoundedAgent is not in production path.** src/api/routes.py uses verify_mandate() directly via X-Mandate header. The "agent" in the production flow is whatever HTTP client presents the X-Mandate + X-Device-Id + X-User-Id headers — there is no Python-side agent enforcement of the 7-action allowlist on the server. The allowlist only runs client-side (in the demo). If the user's intent was server-side enforcement of "agent can only call N APIs", that's NOT what's happening — the server enforces mandate bounds but does NOT gate which actions the calling agent may attempt. Consider documenting this distinction explicitly in ARCHITECTURE.md or moving BoundedAgent into src/api/ as middleware.
   812→
   813→3. **In-memory cumulative counters (mandates.py:47-49 _cumulative_monthly / _cumulative_24h / _last_activity) are NOT persisted.** They reset on every process restart, which means the ₹15,000/month cap and the 6-month inactivity auto-revoke are effectively unenforced across restarts. mandates.py:43-46 acknowledges this: "A real Postgres-backed implementation (Track E Day 2) would persist these in a ``mandate_counters`` table". Until that lands, the monthly cap and auto-revoke are real within a single process but reset on redeploy. The orchestrator should either (a) bump Track E Day 2 to add a `mandate_counters` table + migration, or (b) explicitly document this limitation in the architecture doc.
   814→
   815→## Surprising findings
   816→
   817→- The 24h cooling-period test (test_upi_circle_cooling_period) is cleverly written: it lowers `cooling_24h_inr=1000` and uses ₹1,500 then ₹500 to fire the gate without needing ₹5,000. This is real test design, not a stub.
   818→- The `simulate_inactivity` helper (mandates.py:306-320) is a clean test affordance — backdates `_last_activity` for a given mandate ID. Good engineering, not a hack.
   819→- `decode_mandate` (mandates.py:280-291) is correctly marked "Never use this for authorization decisions" — the codebase distinguishes inspect-only from verify paths.
   820→- routes.py has a SECOND verify_mandate call at line 1623 (in /v1/simulate) that passes `device_id=None, user_id=None` — explicitly skipping UPI Circle device/user validation in the "what-if" explorer. Comment at lines 1626-1629 owns this trade-off. Honest code.
   821→- The "63/63 tests pass" claim is stale — actual count is 117 passed + 8 skipped (likely Redis/Jaeger-dependent tests skipped in test env without those services).

   880→Specific gaps for orchestrator:
   881→1. **`/v1/usage` per-merchant claim is FALSE** (routes.py:1795-1801 docstring admits aggregate-only; merchant_id not wired into /risk/score → audit body)
   882→2. **Dual-control "HMAC chain" claim is FALSE** (routes.py:1415-1418 stores two independent sha256-truncate-16 digests; no HMAC, no chaining between signature_1 and signature_2)
   883→3. **test_merkle_proof_reconstructs_root is DECORATIVE** (test_v3_endpoints.py:155 contains `or True` making the assert always-pass; the follow-up `assert root == _merkle_root(leaves)` is tautological)
   884→4. **register_model in lifespan is Postgres-only** (routes.py:292 — `if settings.is_postgres:` guard; file mode skips it; the original §A item 4 "dead in prod" gap remains for non-Postgres deployments)
   885→5. **/v1/audit/{id}/proof usability gap** (routes.py:1543 — takes `record_id:int` internal SERIAL PK, NOT the user-facing `audit_id` string returned by /risk/score; no API endpoint to convert audit_id → record_id)
   886→6. **Merkle sealing is NOT atomic with audit INSERT** (logger.py:612 commit happens BEFORE sealer.add at line 616; docstring at lines 373-377 + 605-609 claims "atomic with the audit INSERT above" — this is INACCURATE)
   887→7. **Merkle count-OR-time sealing behavior is UNTESTED** — no test exercises MerkleSealer.add() to verify count threshold OR time threshold triggers a seal; no test verifies prev_interval_root chain; no test verifies the proof() method's actual output structure in Postgres mode
   888→8. **test_db.py 6 tests SKIP by default** — without DATABASE_URL=postgresql://... the entire Postgres-path coverage is invisible (the dual-mode fallback is real but Postgres-path correctness is unverified in CI)
   889→
   890→Surprising findings:
   891→- The "HMAC chain" language in the prior session's worklog is not just unimplemented — there's no HMAC anywhere in the override path; the two admin keys are validated via plain API-key check_key lookup, then stored as independent sha256-truncate-16 digests. So even the redaction is sha256 (not HMAC). The chain exists only via the audit hash chain (raw_hash = sha256(canonical(body) + prev_hash)) — which is shared with every other audit record, not specific to the dual-control override.
   892→- test_merkle_proof_reconstructs_root's `or True` is the kind of thing that suggests the test author hit a bug in the proof builder (the position bookkeeping in MerkleSealer.proof at logger.py:282-304 has a known asymmetry — sibling_idx parity at line 285 vs the proof.append at line 287-295 mixes left/right handling), couldn't make the test pass cleanly, and reached for `or True` as a placeholder. Worth flagging to the orchestrator — the proof builder's position bookkeeping may be wrong for odd leaf indices.
   893→- The simulate endpoint body (routes.py:1620-1766) duplicates the entire /risk/score decision pipeline (~140 lines). The comment at line 1615-1619 admits this is intentional ("extracting a shared helper would touch Track C's decision section") — defensible for a hackathon but a maintenance liability.
   894→- test_db.py:80-81 swallows ALL alembic failures with `except Exception: pass # pragma: no cover — assume schema is already applied`. So if `alembic upgrade head` fails for ANY reason (broken migration, bad DSN, perms), the tests silently proceed and likely fail later with confusing table-not-found errors rather than a clear migration error.

   977→Honest verdict: **6 of 7 claims are REAL; 1 is STUB; 0 are DECORATIVE; 0 are MISSING.**
   978→
   979→The single STUB is Claim #2 — `calibrate_probabilities()` (Bahnsen Eq.(6)) is fully implemented (cost_optimizer.py:259-344), mathematically correct, and unit-tested, but is **imported in routes.py:32 and NEVER CALLED** anywhere in the live API path. It is dead code. If SMOTE/under-sampling is ever wired into the training pipeline (per its own docstring's instructions), the live `optimal_decision()` path would still use the inflated raw probabilities — the calibration step that Bahnsen Eq.(6) is supposed to undo would never fire.
   980→
   981→Top-3 specific gaps for orchestrator to address:
   982→
   983→1. **`calibrate_probabilities()` is dead code** — file: `src/api/routes.py:32` (import), `src/business/cost_optimizer.py:259` (definition). Either (a) wire `proba = calibrate_probabilities(proba, p_orig, p_und)` into the live decision path (with `p_und`/`p_orig` derived from the trained model's metadata + the original training prior — would require storing those priors in the model artifact), or (b) remove the import to avoid the decorative-API smell. Currently the API surface pretends to do Bahnsen Eq.(6) recalibration when it doesn't.
   984→
   985→2. **The headline Bahnsen Eq.(5) per-amount FN cost does NOT influence the primary ACCEPT/REVIEW/REJECT decision** — file: `src/api/routes.py:566` calls `optimal_decision(proba, **DEFAULT_COST_WEIGHTS)` WITHOUT `amount_inr=order.amount_inr`. The 3-way path uses `c_fn=600` constant; only the 5-way `optimal_intervention(proba, order.amount_inr)` at routes.py:578 gets the per-amount FN cost. This is documented as intentional (Track N worklog line 661: "the 3-way optimal_decision() call in routes.py KEEPS the constant c_fn=600... backward compat with Track C's tests"), but it means the cost-optimal ACCEPT/REVIEW/REJECT is the same for a ₹600 order and a ₹52,000 order at the same probability — the opposite of the Bahnsen 2013 paper's headline. The `amount_inr` parameter exists in `optimal_decision()` signature (cost_optimizer.py:93) but is NEVER PASSED in the live 3-way call. Recommend either: (a) pass `amount_inr=order.amount_inr` to the 3-way `optimal_decision` call (would require updating Track C tests that assert `cost_breakdown[action] == optimal_decision(p)[1][action]` without amount_inr), or (b) explicitly document this limitation in API_SPEC.md.
   986→
   987→3. **Dashboard uses `n_resamples=100`, not the ≥500 default the endpoint honors** — file: `dashboard/index.html:111` — `fetch("/v1/policy/cost-curves?n_resamples=100", ...)`. The endpoint default at routes.py:103 is `DEFAULT_COST_CURVE_RESAMPLES = 500` and the bootstrap_cost_ci docstring (cost_optimizer.py:467) explicitly says "≥500 recommended by Drummond-Holte skill.yaml." The production dashboard opts for 100 (probably for the 3-5 sec → 0.6 sec latency trade-off). Either (a) bump the dashboard to n=500 with a loading spinner, or (b) document that the user-facing dashboard uses 100 for UX and only programmatic consumers get the 500 default. The "≥500 bootstrap CIs" claim is true at the endpoint level but misleading at the dashboard UX level.
   988→
   989→Surprising findings:
   990→
   991→- **The 3-way `optimal_decision()` is NOT given `amount_inr` in the live path** — `routes.py:566` and `routes.py:1701` both call `optimal_decision(proba, **DEFAULT_COST_WEIGHTS)` without `amount_inr`. So per-amount FN cost (Bahnsen Eq.(5)) influences ONLY the 5-way `intervention` recommendation (routes.py:578, 1705), NOT the primary ACCEPT/REVIEW/REJECT decision. The Bahnsen 2013 paper's headline (per-amount FN cost) is half-wired at best.
   992→
   993→- **The static `ACCEPT_T, REJECT_T = 0.15, 0.60` constants are NOT removed** — they're shadowed (defined at routes.py:72, surfaced at routes.py:750-751 as `legacy_accept_t`/`legacy_reject_t` for backward-compat in the `gate_thresholds` response field). The worklog claim "replaces static 0.15/0.60" is real in the sense that the decision path no longer consults them; the constants themselves still exist in the source. This is a quibble, not a defect — the comment at routes.py:744 makes the intent explicit.
   994→
   995→- **Track N's `optimal_intervention` tests DO assert real argmin math, not just shape** — `test_optimal_intervention_at_high_p` (test_ship.py:457-498) is particularly strong: it verifies both (a) default weights → otp_verify wins (because eff_otp=0.82 > eff_hold_residual=0.30), AND (b) re-tuned weights with c_hold=1 + residual=0.01 → hold wins. This proves the argmin math is genuinely conditional on weights, not hardcoded. `test_per_amount_fn_cost_changes_optimal_intervention` (test_ship.py:501-519) verifies the same p produces different interventions at different amounts — the Bahnsen Eq.(5) headline property.
   996→
   997→- **The dashboard fetches with `n_resamples=100`, not 500** — `dashboard/index.html:111`. The endpoint's default is 500 (routes.py:103), and the bootstrap_cost_ci docstring says ≥500 is recommended per Drummond-Holte skill.yaml. The dashboard's 100 is a UX trade-off (faster load), but it means the user-facing cost-curve explorer is showing wider CIs than the endpoint's default would.
   998→
   999→- **117/125 tests pass** (8 skipped are Postgres-path test_db.py + Redis-path test_streaming.py). The worklog claimed "105/105 pass" at Track L Day 4 — that was an older state. Current count is 117 passed + 8 skipped = 125 total test functions. The Track N "12 new tests" claim is accurate (test_ship.py grew by 12 tests covering optimal_intervention + per-amount FN cost + intervention_costs in response + intervention_curves endpoint + amount_inr param + 422 validation).

  1053→### T1.4 — Alembic migration 003_mandate_counters.py
  1054→- Created alembic/versions/003_mandate_counters.py (152 lines) following the exact style of 001/002:
  1055→  * revision = "003", down_revision = "002" (annotated assignment form).
  1056→  * CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mandate_counters (mandate_sub TEXT PK, cumulative_monthly NUMERIC(14,2) DEFAULT 0, last_activity_ts BIGINT, updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()).
  1057→  * CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS mandate_counter_events (id BIGSERIAL PK, mandate_sub TEXT, ts BIGINT, amount_inr NUMERIC(14,2), created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()) — append-only 24h-window event log so the rolling cooling predicate is a SQL range filter, not a JSONB array.
  1058→  * CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_mandate_counter_events_sub_ts ON mandate_counter_events (mandate_sub, ts DESC) — covers the 24h cooling-range scan.
  1059→  * CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ix_mandate_counter_events_ts ON mandate_counter_events (ts) — covers the probabilistic cleanup DELETE WHERE ts < now - 86400.
  1060→  * downgrade() drops indexes first, then events table, then counters table (reverse FK order), all with IF EXISTS guards.
  1061→  * Idempotent re-run (CREATE IF NOT EXISTS / DROP IF EXISTS) per the project's established migration convention.
  1062→- Parsed migration file with ast.parse — confirms revision="003", down_revision="002", branch_labels=None, depends_on=None.

  1064→### T1.4 — mandates.py DB read/write with file-mode fallback
  1065→- Added module-level lazy psycopg connection cache (_counters_conn: Any = None) + _counters_conn_lock = threading.Lock() — pattern mirrors src/audit/logger.py:366 (psycopg.connect(db_url, autocommit=False)).
  1066→- Added _get_counters_conn() — returns None when DATABASE_URL unset OR not a Postgres DSN OR psycopg not importable; otherwise opens + caches one shared connection per process. The import is side-effect-free (lazy on first call) so file-mode tests never touch psycopg.
  1067→- Added _reset_counters_conn() — test helper to drop the cached connection (called by reset_upi_counters() so DATABASE_URL flips between tests are picked up).
  1068→- Added _read_db_counters(mandate_sub, now) — returns (cumulative_monthly, last_activity_ts, recent_24h_events). Returns (0.0, -1.0, []) sentinel on no-DB or any DB error (never raises — verify path must degrade to in-memory fallback rather than fail the request).
  1069→- Added _write_db_counters(mandate_sub, *, new_cumulative_monthly, last_activity_ts, txn_ts, txn_amount) — UPSERTs mandate_counters row + appends a mandate_counter_events row in one transaction (commit); on any error rolls back + swallows (verify path has already updated in-memory dicts so the request still succeeds).
  1070→- Modified verify_mandate() — at the START of the upi_circle_delegation path, reads DB counters; if db_last_activity >= 0 (DB row exists) uses db values, else falls back to in-memory dicts. The 6 OC-201B checks (inactivity, per-txn cap, monthly cap, device_id, user_id, 24h cooling) then run against the unified `cumulative_monthly` / `last_act` / `recent` variables. At the END on VALID verdict only, calls _write_db_counters() to persist the new cumulative_monthly + last_activity_ts + the 24h cooling event. BREACH/REVIEW/EXPIRED do NOT advance counters (rejected txns don't consume the monthly cap; REVIEW txns don't re-trigger the cooling window) — same semantics as the in-memory path.
  1071→- Updated reset_upi_counters() — wipes BOTH surfaces: in-memory dicts (always) + Postgres mandate_counters/mandate_counter_events rows (TRUNCATE/DELETE) when DATABASE_URL is set, plus drops the cached connection. On any DB error (tables not yet created — e.g. CI without DATABASE_URL fixture) silently falls through to the in-memory clear.

  1149→  * Added `import time` (line 73) — needed for `time.time()` wall-clock ts in detection-delay metric.
  1150→  * Added module-level `_combined_state(ddm_state, adwin_state)` helper (lines 89-98) — combines DDM + ADWIN states into one ordinal signal (DRIFT > WARNING > STABLE). A transition in EITHER detector is treated as a system-level transition for the Gama §5 metric emission.
  1151→  * Extended `LabelFeedbackService.__init__()` with optional `metrics: Any = None` parameter (line 121). None default preserves the existing test contract (test_feedback.py constructs the service without a metrics arg) — the observe_summary calls become no-ops. 11-routes will pass `state["metrics"]` in the production lifespan.
  1152→  * Added three private state fields for transition detection (lines 174-176 + 156-176 docstring):
  1153→    - `_window_start_ts: float | None = None` — set on the first sample after init/reset; used as the t0 for `delta_ts` in the detection-delay metric (Gama §5 "Time to Detection" / MTTD).
  1154→    - `_warning_sample_count: int = 0` — number of samples accumulated while the combined state was in WARNING; emitted as the false-alarm run length (Gama §5 "ARL_0": Average Run Length between false alarms) when WARNING reverts to STABLE without escalating to DRIFT.
  1155→    - `_prev_combined_state: str = "STABLE"` — the combined state from the previous ingest_label call, used for transition detection.
  1156→  * Reworked `ingest_label()` (lines 221-307) to capture `prev_ddm_state`/`prev_adwin_state` BEFORE the `update()` call, compute `prev_combined` vs `new_combined`, and emit:
  1157→    - On `WARNING entered (prev != WARNING)`: reset `_warning_sample_count = 1`.
  1158→    - On `WARNING continues (prev == WARNING)`: increment `_warning_sample_count`.
  1159→    - On `DRIFT entered (prev != DRIFT)`: call `observe_summary("rto_drift_detection_delay_seconds", now_ts - _window_start_ts)` — the wall-clock seconds from the first sample in the current detection window to the drift detection point. Only fires if `self._metrics is not None` and `_window_start_ts is not None`.
  1160→    - On `WARNING reverted to STABLE (prev == WARNING, new == STABLE)`: call `observe_summary("rto_drift_false_alarm_run_length", _warning_sample_count)` — the number of samples accumulated in WARNING before reverting. Reset `_warning_sample_count = 0` after emitting.
  1161→    - On `drift_detected`: existing `_trigger_shadow_retrain` call + DDM/ADWIN reset preserved; ADDITIONALLY reset `_window_start_ts = None`, `_warning_sample_count = 0`, `_prev_combined_state = "STABLE"` so the next detection window starts fresh (Gama §4 "after adaptation, re-establish the baseline from the new concept").
  1162→  * The `_combined_state()` helper is module-level (not on the class) so it's testable directly without instantiating the service.
  1163→- Verified T2.4 end-to-end with a StatefulFakeDetector test harness that lets me control DDM/ADWIN state transitions:
  1164→  * False alarm path (STABLE→WARNING→WARNING→STABLE): `rto_drift_false_alarm_run_length` emitted with value=2 (count of WARNING samples). No `rto_drift_detection_delay_seconds` emitted.
  1165→  * DRIFT detection path (STABLE→DRIFT with 50ms sleep): `rto_drift_detection_delay_seconds` emitted with value≈0.05s. No false-alarm metric emitted.
  1166→  * Escalation path (STABLE→WARNING→WARNING→DRIFT): `rto_drift_detection_delay_seconds` emitted; `rto_drift_false_alarm_run_length` NOT emitted (correct — the WARNING escalated to DRIFT, didn't revert).
